Next it injects an out-of-order two-fragment `IPv6 → Fragment → [one/two padding-only Destination Options] → TCP RST+ACK` containing a nonempty trailing payload; it then sends a SYN with a new sequence number using the **same source/destination addresses and ports** to test reset cleanup and tuple recovery. Every injected TCP segment has a valid original pseudo-header checksum. The 12-case matrix per TUN covers one/two Destination Options headers, eight/24 TCP bytes in the first fragment and option bytes containing valid NOPs, zero-length MSS or zero-length unknown options. The raw TCP kernel's unsolicited RSTs are suppressed in an ephemeral Office network namespace by a temporary nftables rule matching direct IPv6 TCP next headers, **not** the probe's IPv6-fragment next header, and the table is cleaned in `finally`. Because an intentionally ignored RST can leave an older connection alive, the test-only target echo server now serves each accepted TCP socket in its own daemon thread rather than blocking the listener while another case runs. Both fixes concern only the Linux namespace fixture, not production networking.

The first observation showed that Chimera would terminate and reopen the tuple for valid NOP options but **not** for malformed MSS or unknown options, whereas pinned Xray terminates and reopens the same tuple for all three cases; neither implementation echoed duplicate retransmitted data. In Chimera, smoltcp rejects the malformed TCP RST options before handling its reset flag. The existing Xray-only `normalize_xray_fragmented_ipv6_tcp_options` already checks the *original* TCP checksum, the IPv6/TCP sizes, the TCP Data Offset and bounded option parsing before replacing the malformed suffix with inert NOPs and recomputing the checksum. Its exact flag allowlist is now extended to **RST+ACK (`0x14`)** alongside SYN, ACK and FIN+ACK. It is still called *only after IPv6 reassembly* in the opt-in Xray TUN TCP packet pump. **Bare RST, other TCP flag mixes, unfragmented TCP and Chimera-only `tun-gateway` remain unchanged.** This is a narrowly evidenced compatibility correction rather than a permissive global TCP option parser.

The live `run_fragmented_ipv6_tcp_active_rst_reuse_probe` now asserts no duplicate data and successful SYN/SYN-ACK recovery on the same tuple. Vendor integration regression `fragmented_ipv6_active_tcp_rst_malformed_options_allow_xray_tuple_reuse` covers the 12-case matrix in both Xray-opt-in and default stacks, verifying that only the opt-in malformed RST+ACK can release/reopen the tuple; a valid RST+ACK still works in both modes. Verification: the full `XRAY_BIN=./xray bash scripts/test_xray_tun_routes_netns.sh` passed all **12/12 Chimera and 12/12 Xray** active-RST cases with no duplicate echo and successful same-tuple SYN recovery, plus its existing dual-stack, XHTTP/H3, ICMP/PMTU, route rollback and shutdown probes. `cargo test --manifest-path vendor/watfaq-netstack/Cargo.toml` passed (20 unit, 50 integration), and `cargo test -p chimera_server_lib --lib --all-features tun_gateway --locked` passed (40). Strict Vendor/workspace all-target/all-feature Clippy, Rust formatting, Bash syntax, `git diff --check` and reduced-feature `xray-tun` app check passed (pre-existing reduced-feature warnings remain). The results do not prove behavior with out-of-window RST, RST without ACK, TCP challenge ACK, packet loss, retransmission timeouts, resource-limit churn, or arbitrary extension sequences, so Xray TUN remains **Partial**.

### 13.99 Established IPv6 TCP out-of-window and bare RST option compatibility (2026-10-09)

The oracle remains local pinned Xray v26.9.9 (`52a412d9e2f5c2a5142b1b4e2ab3771dacb8b120`), not arbitrary RFC-derived TCP behavior. The 13.98 established-flow raw-socket Linux network-namespace probe is extended to separate three reset cases while preserving the same 12-case axis per implementation: one or two padding-only IPv6 Destination Options after Fragment; eight or 24 TCP bytes in fragment zero; and valid NOP, zero-length MSS or zero-length unknown four-byte TCP options. After a real handshake and successful data echo, a **fragmented RST+ACK with TCP sequence number 2²⁰ bytes beyond the next expected client sequence** is injected with its original correct TCP checksum. The probe records whether any candidate ACK is sent, then sends additional valid TCP data and requires the same active connection to echo those bytes. Only then does it send an *in-window* fragmented RST+ACK and confirm that a new SYN from the **same source/destination IPv6 address/port four-tuple** receives a SYN-ACK. After this reopened handshake, a separate *in-window fragmented bare RST* (flags `0x04`, ACK flag unset) with identical TCP-option variants is injected; a third SYN on the same tuple checks whether this reset, too, frees the connection. The diagnostic fixture uses an ephemeral per-network-namespace nftables rule to suppress **kernel-generated** direct TCP RST packets without filtering the intentionally fragmented test packets, and closes the rule table reliably; target Echo accepts each TCP socket independently. This validates active-connection reset semantics and app-data continuity, not just the lack of a response from an unopened port.

The first live comparison showed Chimera and pinned Xray both **ignore the far-out-of-window RST+ACK** (no candidate challenge ACK observed at this large positive offset) and continue to echo TCP data. Both accept the *in-window* RST+ACK (including malformed options) and permit same-tuple SYN recovery, as fixed in 13.98. However, Chimera only accepted a **bare RST** if options were valid; malformed MSS or unknown option lengths stopped the strict smoltcp parser before reset processing. Pinned Xray accepted bare RST for all three option cases and reopened the same tuple. The narrowly scoped Xray TUN IPv6 fragment-reassembly TCP-option normalizer therefore also allows exact flags **`0x04` (bare RST)** in addition to its previously verified SYN/ACK/FIN+ACK/RST+ACK cases. As before, it validates the *original* TCP checksum and TCP Data Offset, preserves valid option prefixes, replaces only a malformed suffix with inert NOP bytes, and runs **only after IPv6 reassembly in the opt-in `xray-tun` TCP pump**. Unfragmented TCP, default `tun-gateway`, and other TCP flag combinations are not changed.

The live probe now asserts: no duplicate echoed data after retransmission, no candidate ACK for this far-out-of-window RST, the established flow survives and echoes subsequent data, same-tuple recovery after in-window RST+ACK, and same-tuple recovery after bare RST. The vendored `fragmented_ipv6_active_tcp_rst_malformed_options_allow_xray_tuple_reuse` integration test now exercises **both RST flags** and a far-out-of-window RST followed by application-layer data read, in the opt-in and default constructors; default parsing continues to reject malformed TCP options even though it accepts valid bare RST. Final verification: `XRAY_BIN=./xray bash scripts/test_xray_tun_routes_netns.sh` passed **12/12 cases for Chimera and 12/12 for pinned Xray** under all five asserted properties, alongside the existing dual-stack TCP/UDP, XHTTP/H3, ICMP/PMTU, route rollback and shutdown cleanup probes. `cargo test --manifest-path vendor/watfaq-netstack/Cargo.toml` passed (20 unit, 50 integration), and `cargo test -p chimera_server_lib --lib --all-features tun_gateway --locked` passed (40). Both Vendor and full-workspace all-target/all-feature Clippy with `-D warnings`, Rust formatting, Bash syntax, `git diff --check`, and the reduced-feature `xray-tun` app check passed (the app still reports its 33 pre-existing reduced-feature warnings). No commit was made and the pre-existing dirty tree was preserved. This does not establish Challenge ACK behavior for near/in-window non-exact sequence numbers, rate limiting, other TCP flag combinations, or long-term resource reclamation; Xray TUN stays **Partial**.

### 14.00 Established IPv6 fragmented RST one-byte window boundaries (2026-10-09)

The fixed reference remains `ref/xray-core` Xray **26.9.9**, commit `52a412d9e2f5c2a5142b1b4e2ab3771dacb8b120`, binary `26.9.9 Custom (go1.27rc2 linux/amd64)`. An extension to the existing real Linux namespace raw-TCP fixture exercises **two near-window non-exact RST+ACK sequence positions** after an actual IPv6 TCP handshake: *one byte below* the next expected client sequence (`RCV.NXT - 1`), and *one byte above* (`RCV.NXT + 1`, within the advertised receive window). The probe constructs checksum-correct out-of-order two-fragment IPv6 packets with a **nonempty trailing TCP RST payload**, one/two padding-only Destination Options headers, eight/24 bytes of TCP header in fragment zero and valid NOP/malformed MSS/malformed unknown option bytes. It uses fresh fragment IDs and existing same-tuple SYN recovery after prior exact-sequence RST+ACK and bare RST to establish a third session before these near-sequence tests. The -1 RST is followed by **new valid application data that must still be echoed**; only if that succeeds is the +1 RST sent, after which a new SYN on the **same tuple must succeed**. A separately observed candidate pure ACK with the old connection's expected receive sequence records Challenge ACK behavior, rather than conflating an ACK with connection termination. This repeats all **12 cases per implementation** and preserves earlier duplicate-retransmission, very-far-out-of-window RST, exact RST+ACK and bare RST checks.

Pinned Xray/gVisor **ignores the -1 RST** even though the TCP wire segment has trailing bytes; the established connection remains alive and successfully echoes subsequent data. It **accepts the +1 RST** (a non-exact sequence inside the receive window), releases the existing TCP session and permits tuple reuse with SYN/SYN-ACK. In this test, pinned Xray emits **no candidate Challenge ACK** for either -1 or +1. Unmodified Chimera's smoltcp parser incorrectly aborted the connection on -1 RST *even with valid NOP options*, because its `Socket::process` sequence-window overlap check counts trailing TCP payload bytes, whereas the gVisor `handleReset` routine calls `rcv.acceptable(s.sequenceNumber, 0)`, treating RST as a **zero-length** window validation. The resulting premature close was directly reproduced with Chimera's actual Linux TUN and the follow-up echo failed. This is a connection-state correctness bug, not merely a cosmetic or TCP option-parsing mismatch.

The opt-in `vendor/watfaq-netstack/src/tcp_listener.rs` `normalize_xray_fragmented_ipv6_tcp_options` now **validates the original IPv6 TCP pseudo-header checksum first**, then, *only for reassembled IPv6 RST (`0x04`) or RST+ACK (`0x14`)*, strips trailing TCP data, adjusts the reassembled IPv6 payload length and pseudo-header TCP length and recomputes the TCP checksum before smoltcp observes the packet. Existing malformed-option suffix replacement with inert NOP bytes still applies when needed and remains limited to the original exact flag allowlist (SYN, ACK, FIN+ACK, bare RST and RST+ACK). For RST packets with valid TCP options, the same payload stripping is required; restricting it to malformed options would leave a safety-relevant discrepancy. This does **not** alter transmitted packets from the real network, data-carrying ACK/FIN+ACK, unfragmented RST, ordinary `tun-gateway`, or other unverified flag combinations. A vendored integration regression now also constructs a checksum-valid -1 RST with trailing payload in the Xray opt-in stack, injects it before a valid application data segment and verifies the established stream still reads the expected data, while keeping the original far-out-of-window and same-tuple reset coverage. Final validation passed: `XRAY_BIN=./xray bash scripts/test_xray_tun_routes_netns.sh` completed **12/12 Chimera and 12/12 pinned-Xray cases** with the connection-state assertions, as well as the pre-existing dual-stack TCP/UDP, XHTTP/H3, ICMP/PMTU, route rollback and shutdown-cleanup checks. The observed -1 RST ACK difference was recorded on **all 12 cases** (Chimera emitted an ACK; Xray did not), while both sides stayed alive and both sides accepted +1 resets. `cargo test --manifest-path vendor/watfaq-netstack/Cargo.toml` passed (20 unit, 50 integration), and `cargo test -p chimera_server_lib --lib --all-features tun_gateway --locked` passed (40). Strict Vendor and workspace all-target/all-feature Clippy with `-D warnings`, Rust formatting, Bash syntax, `git diff --check` and reduced-feature `xray-tun` app build also passed (with the existing 33 reduced-feature warnings). No commits were made and the pre-existing dirty tree was preserved.

**Remaining measured mismatch:** After the safer zero-length handling, Chimera's smoltcp emits a pure ACK for the -1 RST (`challenge_ack_reply` handles `RCV.NXT-1`), but pinned Xray/gVisor did not emit a matching ACK in this probe. Both now agree on the important connection-state outcome: **-1 remains alive, +1 closes**. The real TUN probe records these ACK differences but does not assert ACK parity. No unsafe sequence-number rewrite, blanket ACK suppression, or global TCP stack behavior change was introduced to hide the remaining difference. This one-byte boundary behavior does not establish general TCP Challenge ACK rate limiting or all receive-window positions; **Xray TUN remains Partial**.

### 14.01 Repeated near-window RST Challenge ACK timing and original-checksum guard (2026-10-09)

The pinned source of external behavior is still **Xray 26.9.9**, commit `52a412d9e2f5c2a5142b1b4e2ab3771dacb8b120`, using its bundled Linux gVisor TUN stack. The established-flow raw IPv6 TCP Linux namespace fixture from 14.00 now contains a **small timing control** in two representative paths per implementation: one vs two padding-only IPv6 Destination Options headers after Fragment, each with 8 TCP header bytes in fragment zero and a valid four-byte NOP option field. A real third TCP connection has first been established through the same tuple-reuse sequence used by the previous probes. The fixture sends a checksum-valid, out-of-order, **two-fragment RST+ACK** at exactly `RCV.NXT - 1` with nonempty trailing TCP data and observes any pure ACK with the correct TCP acknowledgment number; sends the same RST at that sequence with a fresh fragment ID within about 120 ms; waits **1.12 seconds**; then sends a third otherwise equivalent RST+ACK containing **no TCP payload at all** (a nonempty second fragment is still possible because the 24-byte TCP header is split after 8 TCP bytes). Each read is tuple-matched and verifies the received TCP checksum. Afterward the same established session must echo additional valid data, while the original 12-case / implementation matrix still asserts no duplicate application echo, far-out-of-window RST survival, same-tuple recovery after in-window bare RST and RST+ACK, survival at `RCV.NXT - 1`, and recovery following `RCV.NXT + 1`.

The measured **Chimera** response on each of the two timing controls is: first -1 RST **pure ACK**, immediate duplicate **no second ACK**, after the 1.12-second interval **ACK again** for the header-only RST. The installed smoltcp implementation uses `challenge_ack_reply` with a per-socket **one-second rate limiter**, which is consistent with that observation. The pinned **Xray/gVisor** returned **no pure ACK** at any of these three points on either extension layout, including after one second without trailing TCP payload. Its `Endpoint.handleReset` checks `rcv.acceptable(s.sequenceNumber, 0)` and returns without sending an ACK when the reset is unacceptable; this narrow source and live observation explains why simply waiting or dropping trailing RST bytes does not align reply semantics. Crucially, **both stacks preserved the connection and echoed the subsequent payload**. The live test deliberately *records* this remaining response divergence rather than asserting false exact-response parity or suppressing normal TCP ACK traffic. The timing controls are not a proof of global per-source/per-second rate limiting, challenge ACK behavior after other TCP flags, or broader receive-window points.

The vendored regression `fragmented_ipv6_active_tcp_rst_malformed_options_allow_xray_tuple_reuse` was separately strengthened with a **corrupt-original-checksum exact-sequence RST** in each Xray-opt-in case, covering normal NOP and malformed TCP options, bare RST and RST+ACK, one/two Destination Options chains and 8/24 TCP first-fragment lengths. It flips a trailing byte *after* calculating the original TCP checksum and confirms the connection remains alive by reading application data before the later valid in-window RST is sent. This is especially relevant because the Xray-only normalizer may rewrite malformed TCP option lengths and remove trailing RST data: neither operation is permitted to make an **originally invalid checksum** RST effective. No production runtime implementation changes are made in 14.01. Validation passed: full `XRAY_BIN=./xray bash scripts/test_xray_tun_routes_netns.sh` including **12/12 Chimera and 12/12 pinned-Xray** active-RST cases, two additional repeat-timing control cases per implementation, original dual-stack forwarding, H3, ICMP/PMTU, route rollback and shutdown cleanup; `cargo test --manifest-path vendor/watfaq-netstack/Cargo.toml` (20 unit, 50 integration); `cargo test -p chimera_server_lib --lib --all-features tun_gateway --locked` (40); strict Vendor/workspace all-feature Clippy; formatting, Bash syntax, `git diff --check`; and reduced-feature `xray-tun` app build (the existing 33 reduced-feature warnings remain). No Git commit or workspace cleanup occurred. **Xray TUN stays Partial** for exact Challenge ACK reply parity; dropping outbound ACKs in the shared packet pump risks deleting legitimate application/data ACKs and is not justified by these two controls.

### 14.02 Established IPv6 TCP invalid ACK sequence and acknowledgment-number matrix (2026-10-09)

Pinned source: Xray `26.9.9 Custom (go1.27rc2 linux/amd64)`, `ref/xray-core` commit `52a412d9e2f5c2a5142b1b4e2ab3771dacb8b120`; TUN TCP implementation uses gVisor. The 14.01 experiment established that **RST-specific** unmatched-sequence replies differ; that is not sufficient evidence for altering global ACK treatment. This iteration therefore adds `run_fragmented_ipv6_tcp_active_ack_window_probe` to the real Linux namespace TUN test. Each probe opens a **separate established IPv6 TCP connection** to the echo target by valid raw SYN/SYN-ACK/ACK, echoes a first payload, sends a **checksum-correct, out-of-order two-fragment ACK-only** TCP segment with four valid NOP TCP option bytes and one or two padding-only IPv6 Destination Options headers after Fragment, checks any candidate pure-ACK response against the correct transport tuple, expected acknowledgment number and TCP checksum, then sends a second valid application payload and requires the same TCP flow to echo it unchanged. It finally sends an in-window RST to clean up the test flow. The first IPv6 fragment carries eight TCP header bytes. The four probe cases are: (1) TCP sequence `RCV.NXT-1` with correct ACK number; (2) sequence `RCV.NXT+1` within the receive window; (3) sequence `RCV.NXT+2²⁰` well beyond the window; and (4) **exact** client sequence with an ACK number **64 bytes beyond the server's SYN-ACK-confirmed transmit sequence** (no such server data was sent). Two extension-chain layouts give **eight independent connections for each implementation**. The fixture explicitly drains delayed response packets from the initial echo before injecting each anomalous ACK to avoid misattribution.

The full fixed-Xray and Chimera live comparison measured **eight of eight surviving data flows on each side**; both implementations replied with a pure ACK for `RCV.NXT-1` and the far-out-of-window sequence, and neither replied for `RCV.NXT+1`. An **invalid future acknowledgment number** is the only observed response difference: smoltcp/Chimera emits a pure ACK, while Xray/gVisor **does not** in either extension-chain layout. This aligns with the installed smoltcp `Socket::process` path that calls `challenge_ack_reply` on ACK numbers above its allowed range, whereas the live gVisor reference silently ignores the same test case. This distinction matters: gVisor *does* send ACKs for some unacceptable **non-RST** sequence numbers, so globally suppressing all challenge ACKs to address the 14.01 RST mismatch would actively reduce compatibility.

The new live ACK probe asserts the distinct expected reply per implementation and the echo-before/echo-after data continuity for every case. A focused vendored integration regression `active_ipv6_tcp_future_ack_challenges_but_preserves_stream_data` checks the actual local smoltcp behavior in both default and Xray-opt-in constructors: inject a valid, excessive future ACK number after handshake; expect a pure TCP ACK reply; then verify actual application bytes still reach the established stream. The local test prevents an unreviewed future change from silently removing this acknowledgment or breaking data; **it does not assert that this smoltcp response matches Xray**. No production TCP state-machine/packet-pump logic or default site `tun-gateway` behavior was changed in this iteration. The reference-specific mismatch remains documented **Partial** rather than adding a risky stateless output-ACK filter. Verification passed: `XRAY_BIN=./xray bash scripts/test_xray_tun_routes_netns.sh` asserted **8/8 Chimera and 8/8 pinned-Xray** isolated pure-ACK cases and retained the existing 12/12 per-implementation fragmented RST, established TCP ACK/FIN echo, dual-stack TCP/UDP, XHTTP/H3, ICMP/PMTU, route rollback and shutdown cleanup checks. `cargo test --manifest-path vendor/watfaq-netstack/Cargo.toml` passed **20 unit and 51 integration** tests (including the new future-ACK regression); `cargo test -p chimera_server_lib --lib --all-features tun_gateway --locked` passed **40**. Vendor and workspace all-target/all-feature strict Clippy with `-D warnings`, Rust formatting, Bash syntax, `git diff --check`, and reduced-feature `xray-tun` app check also passed (with the existing 33 reduced-feature warnings). No Git commit or workspace cleanup occurred. Control cases using TCP window scaling, SACK, TCP timestamp options, send-window exhaustion, zero-window probes and rate-limits beyond this matrix are not yet verified.

### 14.03 Established IPv6 TCP zero-window probe and reopening (2026-10-09)

The fixed Xray oracle remains **26.9.9 Custom (go1.27rc2 linux/amd64)** with checked-in reference commit `52a412d9e2f5c2a5142b1b4e2ab3771dacb8b120`. This iteration adds a dedicated Linux network-namespace, raw IPv6 TCP test `run_fragmented_ipv6_tcp_zero_window_recovery_probe`, independent of the invalid-ACK and RST matrices. For each of **one or two padding-only IPv6 Destination Options extension headers** after an IPv6 Fragment header, it creates an established TCP connection with valid SYN/SYN-ACK/ACK, advertises a receive **window of zero** to the TUN-side TCP endpoint, then sends a checksum-valid out-of-order **two-fragment TCP ACK with new application data and an advertised window of zero**. The transport-layer TCP option field contains four NOP bytes and fragment zero carries eight TCP bytes. The proxy forwards the inbound application payload to an actual TCP Echo server, which attempts to send the same data back. The raw test client remains at window zero for **1.7 seconds**, verifies inbound client data was ACKed, inspects TCP reply sequence numbers and verifies each reply TCP pseudo-header checksum; it explicitly checks that no full Echo payload crossed the closed receive window. It sends zero-window ACKs in response to any data probe, without advancing its acknowledgment number. Then it sends a fragmented, checksum-valid **nonzero-window update** (4096 bytes), assembles/ACKs the original Echo payload in sequence, and sends a **second new application payload** to assert the recovered connection remains usable. An in-window fragmented RST terminates each fixture flow and nftables rules used to suppress the Office kernel's unsolicited RST packets are removed in `finally`. No external client TCP API or kernel scheduler is assumed for the peer under test.

The direct pinned Xray vs Chimera comparison showed **the same functional window/backpressure behavior** in both cases per implementation. While the TCP receive window is zero, Chimera/smoltcp sent **one one-byte data probe with sequence equal to the server's first post-SYN byte** (`ISN+1`, offset zero in the trace) during the sample; pinned Xray/gVisor sent **three one-byte probes with sequence `ISN`** (offset -1, the last byte before the stream's first unacknowledged byte) in the same sampling interval. The latter matches gVisor's `sender.sendZeroWindowProbe` implementation, which deliberately probes with `SndUna-1` rather than the next byte. Both TCP stacks held back the complete Echo payload, then sent it correctly after the advertised receive window reopened, and both echoed the second application payload without requiring a new connection. The live test asserts that at least one one-byte probe is observed, the **per-implementation one-byte probe sequence convention**, the inbound-data ACK, absence of premature full payload, recovery of every byte, and continued Echo after recovery. It does **not** demand identical probe retransmission counts or timing, which may vary with retransmission timers.

A focused vendored integration test `active_ipv6_tcp_zero_window_defers_outbound_until_window_update` also runs both the default and opt-in `NetStack` constructors without touching the Xray-only compatibility layer. It handshakes an IPv6 TCP stream, advertises zero window, buffers outbound application bytes on the established netstack stream, checks that no full application payload is emitted during the closed-window observation, then opens the advertised window and checks exact outbound payload delivery. This protects ordinary `tun-gateway` and `xray-tun` against inadvertent flow-control regressions. No production runtime logic has been changed for zero-window handling; the different probe convention does not itself prove a client-visible failure, so replacing smoltcp's shared TCP sender just for trace identity is not justified. Final verification passed: full `XRAY_BIN=./xray bash scripts/test_xray_tun_routes_netns.sh` with **2/2 Chimera and 2/2 pinned-Xray** asserted zero-window probes and recovery, plus the existing established TCP ACK/RST and half-close tests, dual-stack TCP/UDP, XHTTP/H3, ICMP/PMTU, route rollback and shutdown cleanup checks. `cargo test --manifest-path vendor/watfaq-netstack/Cargo.toml` passed (20 unit, 52 integration), `cargo test -p chimera_server_lib --lib --all-features tun_gateway --locked` passed (40), and Vendor/workspace all-target/all-feature strict Clippy, Rust formatting, Bash syntax and `git diff --check` passed. The reduced-feature `xray-tun` app check passed with its existing 33 warnings. No commits or workspace cleanup were performed. There is not yet evidence for full retransmission timeout parity, loss/reorder during reopening, SACK/window scale options, a genuinely stalled peer for minutes, or high-concurrency zero-window resource pressure, so **Xray TUN stays Partial**.

### 14.04 Lost zero-window update fragment, retransmission and duplicate Echo suppression (2026-10-09)

The oracle remains pinned Xray 26.9.9 at commit `52a412d9e2f5c2a5142b1b4e2ab3771dacb8b120`. The 14.03 established raw IPv6 TCP Linux namespace fixture now deliberately drops the *trailing IPv6 fragment* of the first nonzero-window update, forwarding only fragment zero of a checksum-correct TCP ACK advertising window 4096. It retains the zero advertised receive window for an additional 3.2 seconds, continues ACKing one-byte persist probes without advancing RCV.NXT, and requires another one-byte probe and no premature full Echo. The intentionally incomplete fragment ID is never completed. The raw client then resends the *whole* window update in two out-of-order IPv6 fragments with a **fresh** fragment ID. This distinguishes a real missing fragment from TCP's treatment of a complete but unacknowledged window update.

After the reopened window delivers the original Echo, the raw client intentionally *withholds the cumulative ACK for the first data response*. It requires the TCP server to retransmit the already-received data at the **same sequence with identical bytes** within a bounded 4.5-second sample, without appending that duplicate to the application buffer. It then ACKs the retransmission, sends a **second new fragmented application payload**, and requires its Echo at the next sequence number; the flow is closed by an in-window fragmented RST. Both test cases (one/two padding-only IPv6 Destination Options after Fragment, eight TCP bytes in fragment zero) explicitly validate output TCP checksums and tuple matching. The assertions cover acknowledgment of inbound data, one-byte window probes, a retransmitted probe after lost update, no full-data escape while the window remains zero, retransmission after the dropped data ACK, exact byte recovery, and continued stream use.

Real Linux namespace oracle comparison succeeded for **2/2 Chimera and 2/2 pinned Xray** cases. Chimera/smoltcp's one-byte probe uses the first unacknowledged server data sequence (offset 0); pinned Xray/gVisor probes SndUna-1 (offset -1), as already established in 14.03. The test asserts the respective probe shape but not equal retransmission counts or timing. Both recover correctly despite the injected fragment and ACK loss. The vendor integration regression `active_ipv6_tcp_zero_window_defers_outbound_until_window_update` now covers the same incomplete IPv6 window update in *both* default and opt-in stacks, using an intentionally missing second fragment at ID `0x55667721`, then a complete update with fresh ID `0x55667722` in reversed fragment order. Neither full Echo before reassembly nor stream loss after recovery is permitted. No production TCP flow-control code was changed. Final verification: `XRAY_BIN=./xray bash scripts/test_xray_tun_routes_netns.sh` passed with **2/2 Chimera and 2/2 pinned-Xray** asserted lost-update/ACK-loss cases plus existing established TCP ACK/RST, dual-stack TCP/UDP, XHTTP/H3, ICMP/PMTU, route rollback and shutdown checks. `cargo test --manifest-path vendor/watfaq-netstack/Cargo.toml` passed **20 unit and 52 integration**, and `cargo test -p chimera_server_lib --lib --all-features tun_gateway --locked` passed **40**. Vendor/workspace all-target/all-feature strict Clippy with `-D warnings`, Rust formatting, Bash syntax, `git diff --check`, and the reduced-feature `xray-tun` app build passed (with the existing 33 reduced-feature warnings). No Git commit or workspace cleanup was performed. Real packet loss during congestion, multi-minute backoff, SACK, window scaling, many concurrent persist sessions, and all retransmission timer equivalence remain unverified; **Xray TUN stays Partial**.

### 14.05 Extended IPv6 TCP zero-window persist backoff and data recovery (2026-10-09)

Oracle is the same pinned Xray 26.9.9 (`ref/xray-core` commit `52a412d9e2f5c2a5142b1b4e2ab3771dacb8b120`) and the live Linux namespace raw IPv6/TCP fixture from 14.04. This iteration deliberately changes **only the duration of the incomplete window-update scenario** for the two-Destination-Options case, retaining the one-option/short-block baseline for control. Both fixtures first accept client data while advertising zero TCP receive window; the first nonzero-window update is sent with its *second IPv6 fragment deliberately absent*, leaving reassembly incomplete and preventing early opening. The two-option case remains at zero advertised TCP window for approximately **1.7 + 12.0 seconds** rather than 1.7 + 3.2 seconds. The raw client sends a zero-window ACK in reply to each valid one-byte persist probe, verifies all TCP output checksums and tuples, records monotonic probe arrival offsets and checks that the **first two inter-probe gaps grow** rather than assuming both TCP stacks use identical schedules. It then sends a complete fragmented window update with a *new* fragment ID, validates the original Echo is restored exactly, deliberately withholds the first complete-data ACK to exercise a retransmission timer, and sends a second fresh application payload on the same TCP session.

The fixed implementation sources explain the expected probe-shape difference: smoltcp 0.13.1 (`src/socket/tcp.rs`, `Timer::rewind_zero_window_probe`) doubles the zero-window-probe delay after each probe, while gVisor's `sender.sendZeroWindowProbe` retransmits an already-acknowledged byte at sequence `SndUna-1` and rearms its TCP resend timer. In the first direct live long-stall measurement, Chimera sent one-byte probes at roughly **0, 2 and 6 seconds** (2 then 4 seconds between probes) and recovered its Echo and a fresh subsequent payload. The pinned Xray control sent one-byte probes at roughly **0.2, 0.6, 1.4, 3.0, 6.2, 12.6 seconds** (successive gaps of approximately 0.4, 0.8, 1.6, 3.2 and 6.4 seconds), and **also fully recovered both payloads**. However, the initial test's fixed **4.5-second** post-window data-ACK-loss retransmission deadline observed a duplicate retransmission on Chimera but *not* on the pinned Xray long-stall case, even though subsequent fresh Echo succeeded. The follow-up test therefore increases this specific long-stall ACK-loss observation horizon to **18 seconds** and measures actual post-window retransmission delay separately from the primary window recovery result; an absent retransmission by an arbitrary short deadline must not be misreported as a data-path failure. The second full fixed-oracle run confirmed the explanation: Chimera retransmitted the Echo with identical sequence/bytes approximately **1.0 second** after reopening, while pinned Xray did so approximately **12.8 seconds** after reopening in the 14-second blocked case. The short-window Xray control retransmitted after about **3.2 seconds**. **Both** implementations recovered the exact original Echo, ignored duplicates at the application layer, and echoed a fresh subsequent message on the same connection; this is a retransmission-timeout difference, not established data loss.

The vendored integration regression `active_ipv6_tcp_zero_window_defers_outbound_until_window_update` now holds the **incomplete fragmented TCP window update** for about **3.4 seconds per constructor**, instead of observing at most one frame over 200 milliseconds. It continues to check that the TCP payload length never exceeds one byte while the window remains zero; one-byte probes are answered with a zero-window ACK without advancing the acknowledgment number. At least one persist probe must be observed before the complete fresh-ID window update reopens the window and permits the original buffered data. The regression runs in both the default `tun-gateway` constructor and the opt-in Xray mode to keep the common smoltcp backpressure behavior bounded and testable. No production TCP state-machine changes are justified by the observed probe timing differences. Final verification: the full `XRAY_BIN=./xray bash scripts/test_xray_tun_routes_netns.sh` passed with **2/2 Chimera and 2/2 pinned-Xray** zero-window cases including the extended persist/backoff assertion, lost-update fragment, lost data ACK, exact retransmitted TCP bytes and second-message continuation. Its existing active TCP ACK/RST, dual-stack TCP/UDP, XHTTP/H3, ICMP/PMTU, route rollback and shutdown checks also passed. `cargo test --manifest-path vendor/watfaq-netstack/Cargo.toml` passed **20 unit and 52 integration**; `cargo test -p chimera_server_lib --lib --all-features tun_gateway --locked` passed **40**. Vendor and workspace all-target/all-feature strict Clippy with `-D warnings`, Rust formatting, Bash syntax, `git diff --check` and the reduced-feature `xray-tun` app build passed (the pre-existing **33** reduced-feature warnings remain). No Git commit or workspace cleanup was made. Multi-minute zero windows, peer disappearance, scaled TCP windows, SACK, heterogeneous RTT/RTO, large queued writes and resource limit recovery remain outside this slice; **Xray TUN stays Partial**.

### 14.06 Established IPv6 TCP Window Scale negotiation and scaled window reopening (2026-10-09)

The external reference remains the fixed local **Xray 26.9.9** binary (`26.9.9 Custom (go1.27rc2 linux/amd64)`, source `ref/xray-core` commit `52a412d9e2f5c2a5142b1b4e2ab3771dacb8b120`). This iteration adds a **third, independently established TCP connection** to the 14.05 raw IPv6 TCP namespace oracle: one padding-only Destination Options header after the IPv6 Fragment header, with eight TCP header bytes in the first fragment. The two original unscaled connections remain unchanged, including the long zero-window persist/backoff control. In the new case the raw client includes a syntactically valid TCP **Window Scale option kind 3 / length 3 / shift 3** in its SYN, with correct TCP checksum. The fixture parses the server's actual SYN-ACK TCP option bytes, requires that the server also offers a Window Scale option, and records its advertised *local receive* shift independently of the client's shift. The ACK and subsequent non-SYN segments deliberately contain only valid NOP padding, because TCP window scale is negotiated during the SYN exchange, **not** renegotiated on later TCP segments.

The scaled session follows the same zero-window and deterministic incomplete-fragment route as 14.04: complete inbound data in an IPv6-fragmented ACK, a zero advertised receive window, one-byte persist probes, first 4096-window update missing its trailing IPv6 fragment, and follow-up one-byte probes with the window still closed. After that, **instead of reopening fully**, it first sends a *complete fragmented ACK* with TCP advertised window field **1**. Since the client offered WS=3, the sender's correct interpretation is **1 × 2³ = 8 bytes** of new TCP data permitted. The test observes replies during a bounded 550 ms narrow-window interval, validates checksum and tuple, accumulates contiguous new data from the original Echo and **asserts no more than eight bytes escape** while this small window is active. It deliberately does **not** ACK these bytes during the narrow observation (which would slide the right edge of the window); afterward it ACKs the eight-byte prefix while advertising zero window once more. A complete, out-of-order fragmented ACK using a fresh fragment ID then reopens the receive window to 4096 raw units, allowing the remaining Echo payload. The raw client again intentionally withholds the first complete-data ACK and requires an exact-sequence/bytes retransmission, acknowledges without duplicating application data and sends/receives a second fresh application payload on the *same connection*. The existing two unscaled controls still test short and ~14-second persist timers and window recovery without WS.

The fixed-oracle real TUN comparison observed **Chimera's SYN-ACK advertising Window Scale 3**, whereas **pinned Xray's SYN-ACK advertised Window Scale 8**. These are *server-side receive* shifts; their values need not match the client's offered shift 3, which controls the server's **send** window based on client ACK advertisements. Both implementations correctly released **exactly eight bytes** with the client's window field 1 and shift 3, withheld the remaining Echo until the larger window update, and subsequently recovered the original and fresh payloads, including retransmission following the deliberately lost data ACK. Thus **the important send-window semantics agree despite the differing SYN-ACK options**. The test asserts a valid negotiated Window Scale, the eight-byte bound, continuing zero-window probe/fragment-loss behavior, recovered original bytes, duplicate suppression and fresh-data continuation; it does not assert the same local receive buffer, SYN-ACK shift or timer intervals. No production TCP-state-machine changes were warranted.

The vendored integration regression `active_ipv6_tcp_zero_window_defers_outbound_until_window_update` now expands to **four cases**: default and opt-in Xray netstack constructors, each with and without the client's Window Scale 3. It creates a real TCP SYN option using `etherparse::TcpOptionElement::WindowScale(3)`, requires a Window Scale option on SYN-ACK in the scaled case, holds a deliberately incomplete IPv6-fragmented 4096-window update while continuing one-byte persist probes, and verifies a scaled window field of 1 exposes only eight bytes. It then acknowledges those eight while closing the window, sends a full fragmented window update under a fresh ID, and asserts the complete outbound application byte stream. This protects both ordinary site `tun-gateway` and opt-in Xray TUN behavior, without altering their TCP senders or the existing Xray-only fragmented-option normalizer. Final verification: `XRAY_BIN=./xray bash scripts/test_xray_tun_routes_netns.sh` succeeded with **3/3 Chimera and 3/3 pinned-Xray** zero-window/Window Scale cases, including their original short/long persist-backoff, deliberately incomplete IPv6 window-update fragment, suppressed data ACK and exact retransmission, plus all existing active TCP ACK/RST, TCP half-close, dual-stack TCP/UDP, XHTTP/H3, ICMP/PMTU, route rollback and shutdown-cleanup checks. `cargo test --manifest-path vendor/watfaq-netstack/Cargo.toml` passed **20 unit and 52 integration**, and `cargo test -p chimera_server_lib --lib --all-features tun_gateway --locked` passed **40**; strict Vendor and workspace all-target/all-feature Clippy with `-D warnings`, Rust formatting, Bash syntax, `git diff --check`, and the reduced-feature `xray-tun` app check passed (with the existing **33** reduced-feature warnings). No Git commit or workspace cleanup was made. This is **not** comprehensive Window Scale compatibility for all shifts 0–14, mixed MSS/SACK/timestamp options, IPv4, multiple simultaneously stalled connections, receive-direction high-throughput backpressure or long-term memory pressure; **Xray TUN remains Partial**.

### 14.07 Window Scale 0/1/3, fragmented compound SYN with MSS/SACK/Timestamp (2026-10-09)

The external oracle remains the pinned local **Xray 26.9.9 Custom (go1.27rc2 linux/amd64)**, `ref/xray-core` commit `52a412d9e2f5c2a5142b1b4e2ab3771dacb8b120`. This iteration extends the established raw IPv6 TCP Linux network-namespace test without changing production code, the ordinary `tun-gateway`, or the two unscaled baseline TCP streams. The original WS=3 raw SYN persists. Two new connections advertise **WS=0 and WS=1** respectively; the 20-byte-payload Echo is held behind a zero advertised receive window, with one broken first IPv6-fragmented window update, one-byte persist-probe ACKs and a complete fresh-ID update. They first reopen the *client* receive window with TCP raw field **1**: effective server→client send capacity must be `1 << WS`, i.e. **1 byte for WS=0**, **2 bytes for WS=1**, and **8 bytes for the existing WS=3**. The fixture validates the exact number of contiguous bytes, never ACKs them during the narrow window so as not to slide its right edge, then ACKs the prefix with a new zero window, fully opens the window through reassembled IPv6 fragments, confirms exact recovery, deliberately withholds the first full Echo's ACK until a byte-identical TCP retransmission, then verifies a second Echo in the same TCP session. It tracks each negotiated SYN-ACK receive scale independently: the client-advertised scale, not the server's chosen scale, determines the server's send window.

The **WS=1** case additionally sends a **two-fragment, out-of-order SYN** with one/two padding-only IPv6 Destination Options headers after Fragment, where the initial fragment contains only **eight TCP header bytes** and all four **valid SYN-only TCP options** reside partly/wholly in later fragments: MSS 1280 (kind 2, length 4), SACK Permitted (kind 4, length 2), Window Scale shift 1 (kind 3, length 3), and Timestamp (kind 8, length 10), with one NOP to pad the total TCP header to 40 bytes. Original TCP/IPv6 checksums are valid. The fixture parses actual SYN-ACK options to verify WS, MSS and SACK acceptance; it records Timestamp separately, because a TCP peer may omit Timestamp from SYN-ACK and thereby decline timestamp negotiation. Subsequent ACK and fragmented data packets carry a valid Timestamp/TSecr option **only if it was negotiated by both sides**. This matters for PAWS: blindly attaching an unnegotiated timestamp is not a faithful test. The existing independent SYN-ACK and fragmented TCP option tests still cover malformed MSS/options; these new cases check *valid* combinations and send-window semantics. The first real oracle comparison confirmed that **Chimera/smoltcp's SYN-ACK advertises MSS, SACK and local receive WS=3 but no Timestamp** for the compound SYN, whereas **pinned Xray/gVisor advertises MSS, SACK, receive WS=8 and Timestamp**, echoing the offered client TSval in TSecr. Both implementations honor the client's offered WS=1: exactly two data bytes emerge with TCP advertised field 1, the rest is deferred until the full window update, and the original and second Echo are recovered. Xray's compound-Timestamp case, however, did **not** retransmit an intentionally unacknowledged full Echo within the old 4.5-second observation, despite both application payloads succeeding. The control now uses a separate **18-second** retransmission observation for this case, matching the long-persist fixture's distinction between RTO and successful data recovery. Exact cross-stack SYN-ACK local receive scale, timestamp advertisement, and retransmission timers need not be equal to establish correct application data forwarding.

The vendored regression `fragmented_ipv6_tcp_compound_syn_options_keep_stream_usable` creates **eight local cases**: each default and Xray-TUN-compatible constructor receives out-of-order fragments of the valid 40-byte TCP SYN offering MSS 1280, SACK, Timestamp and **WS shifts 0, 1, 7, 14**, then must emit SYN-ACK with MSS/SACK/Window Scale and deliver real application bytes after a correctly formed ACK+data packet using Timestamp only if actually negotiated. The pre-existing `active_ipv6_tcp_zero_window_defers_outbound_until_window_update` remains the separate data-plane regression for WS=3's exact 8-byte window after loss, long zero-window persist probes and late IPv6 fragment recovery. The vendor matrix checks that high shift values remain accepted without claiming a full large-data-window/send-congestion experiment. The installed smoltcp 0.13.1 TCP socket has a disabled timestamp generator by default; an offered Timestamp option alone does not require Chimera to include one in SYN-ACK. These option choices can differ from the pinned Xray stack; any such difference is reported rather than forcing a shared netstack TCP sender change. The complete fixed-oracle second live experiment verified **5/5 Chimera and 5/5 pinned-Xray** independent zero-window connections: two original unscaled controls (short and 14-second persist), WS=3, WS=0 and fragmented compound SYN WS=1; **all five** preserve incomplete-fragment window-update blocking, one-byte probes, exact 1/2/8-byte narrow send windows, full Echo recovery after the complete new-ID update, byte-identical retransmission following withheld ACK, and subsequent fresh Echo. The compound WS=1 Xray case retransmitted about **6.14 seconds** after reopening rather than within the earlier 4.5-second observation; Chimera's corresponding unacknowledged data retransmitted in about **1 second**. This is a transport timer difference, not data loss. The complete namespace fixture's other ACK/RST, IPv4/IPv6 TCP/UDP, H3, ICMP/PMTU, route rollback and cleanup checks are unchanged. Final verification passed: `XRAY_BIN=./xray bash scripts/test_xray_tun_routes_netns.sh` (including **5/5 Chimera and 5/5 pinned-Xray** isolated zero-window/WS cases, with out-of-order fragmented compound SYN), plus the existing active TCP ACK/RST, half-close, IPv4/IPv6 TCP/UDP, XHTTP/H3, ICMP/PMTU, route rollback and clean shutdown. `cargo test --manifest-path vendor/watfaq-netstack/Cargo.toml` passed **20 unit and 53 integration** tests, including the new 8-case compound SYN regression; `cargo test -p chimera_server_lib --lib --all-features tun_gateway --locked` passed **40**. Vendor/workspace strict all-target/all-feature Clippy, Rust formatting, Bash syntax, `git diff --check`, and reduced-feature `xray-tun` app build all passed; the existing **33** reduced-feature warnings remain. No production TCP logic change, Git commit or workspace cleanup was performed. **Xray TUN stays Partial**, especially for large advertised windows, SACK recovery during packet loss, all shifts 0–14 in live TUN, timestamp PAWS/replay, and high-volume transfer.

### 14.08 Established IPv6 TCP receiver SACK, missing segment, reordered fragment and duplicate delivery (2026-10-09)

Pinned oracle: local **Xray 26.9.9 Custom (go1.27rc2 linux/amd64)**, `ref/xray-core` commit `52a412d9e2f5c2a5142b1b4e2ab3771dacb8b120`. This iteration isolates **TCP receiver-side SACK and missing-data recovery**, rather than extending the zero-window persist, Window Scale or Timestamp controls again. The new real Linux namespace fixture `run_fragmented_ipv6_tcp_sack_gap_recovery_probe` opens four independent raw IPv6 TCP flows **per implementation**: one/two padding-only Destination Options after Fragment × SACK-Permitted present/absent in the initial SYN. The raw SYN advertises MSS 1200 and conditionally SACK Permitted. The fixture confirms SYN/SYN-ACK/ACK, injects a complete **out-of-order, two-fragment IPv6 TCP payload segment** with only eight TCP bytes in fragment zero, and deliberately withholds the preceding five application bytes (simulated lost TCP segment). The later segment's IPv6 fragments arrive in reverse order and carry valid original TCP pseudo-header checksum. The receiver's TCP next expected client sequence must remain at the gap: the oracle output must send the unchanged **cumulative ACK** and, **only if SACK was negotiated**, a TCP option kind 5 carrying the exact selective block `[RCV.NXT+5, RCV.NXT+5+tail_len)`. No TCP Echo payload is allowed to escape before the gap is filled. This proves genuine receiver SACK behavior rather than merely the SACK-Permitted bit in SYN-ACK.

The raw client next sends the *missing first TCP segment*, also as two out-of-order IPv6 fragments with a fresh Fragment ID. The now contiguous `missing_prefix + SACKed_tail` must be cumulatively acknowledged and emitted through the existing Echo service **once, in original application-byte order**. The script verifies reply TCP checksums and ports, records the complete Echo in a contiguous sequence buffer without duplicating old retransmissions, requires exact byte equality, sends a **second fresh application payload on the same live flow**, and confirms it is echoed. It then injects a fragmented in-window RST to terminate each fixture connection. The no-SACK control must not emit any SACK block, yet must still buffer and correctly recover out-of-order data (TCP's cumulative-ACK fallback).

The first real namespace reference comparison observed **4/4 Chimera and 4/4 pinned-Xray** flows behaving identically: with SACK advertised, both returned the precise block above the missing prefix, kept the ACK pinned at the prefix and did not deliver premature Echo; with SACK absent, neither generated a SACK block, but both retained the later data until the missing bytes arrived. All eight flows generated an eventual cumulative ACK, recovered the exact full Echo and continued sending a second application message. The live test initially recorded these results, then hardened its assertions to require **SACK block present exactly when negotiated** and absent otherwise, as well as no premature Echo, correct gap-fill recovery and continued data transfer. This is **receiver-side selective acknowledgment plus application ordering**, not evidence that an outbound TCP sender obeys a distant peer's SACK report or recovers efficiently after *outbound* segment loss.

The new vendored integration regression `fragmented_ipv6_tcp_sack_gap_reassembles_once_and_is_negotiated` covers the same **four** constructor/feature combinations (default site `tun-gateway` and opt-in `xray-tun` × SACK negotiated/not negotiated). It sends a SYN with MSS+optional SACK, requires matching SYN-ACK SACK permission, sends later TCP data first as reverse-arriving IPv6 fragments, requires the exact `SelectiveAcknowledgement` option and pinned cumulative ACK in negotiated flows, **asserts the TCP application stream has no bytes before gap repair**, injects the missing TCP prefix, and reads the exact once-reassembled stream. It then retransmits the already-delivered tail as a new IPv6 fragmentation ID and requires that duplicate data **not** enter the application again; a fresh subsequent segment must be delivered normally. Both constructors pass without altering shared TCP handling or the Xray-only IPv6 TCP malformed-options normalizer. The reference gVisor receiver queues out-of-order data and updates its SACK blocks; smoltcp 0.13.1 constructs its `ack_reply` SACK range from the assembler when the remote SYN offered SACK. The narrow test verifies this equivalence but does **not** establish full sender-side selective loss recovery, multi-hole SACK block ordering, DSACK, TCP Timestamp/PAWS or SACK-overflow behavior. **Xray TUN stays Partial**.

Final verification: a fresh, source-frozen `XRAY_BIN=./xray bash scripts/test_xray_tun_routes_netns.sh` **exited 0**, with **4/4 Chimera and 4/4 pinned-Xray** asserted SACK-on/off and chained/non-chained fragment cases, and the existing ACK/RST, TCP FIN, zero-window/WS, dual-stack TCP/UDP, XHTTP/H3, ICMP/PMTU, rollback and clean-shutdown probes all passed. `cargo test --manifest-path vendor/watfaq-netstack/Cargo.toml` passed **20 unit and 54 integration tests**, including the new four-combination SACK/duplicate-data regression; `cargo test -p chimera_server_lib --lib --all-features tun_gateway --locked` passed **40**. Strict Clippy on all Vendor and workspace targets/features, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, Bash syntax and `git diff --check`, plus the reduced-feature app check all passed (existing **33** reduced-feature warnings). An initial live run overlapped an edit to the running shell script and exited with a late shell parse error even after the network checks completed; the script was then frozen, syntax checked and the entire namespace smoke rerun from scratch to a clean exit 0. No production TCP changes, Git commit or worktree cleanup were performed.

### 14.09 Established IPv6 TCP sender SACK feedback and a lost outgoing segment (2026-10-09)

The oracle remains pinned **Xray 26.9.9 Custom (go1.27rc2 linux/amd64)** at `ref/xray-core` commit `52a412d9e2f5c2a5142b1b4e2ab3771dacb8b120`. This iteration isolates the **TCP sender** response to selective acknowledgment, as distinct from the 14.08 receiver-side SACK gap and reassembly matrix. The new Linux-namespace fixture `run_ipv6_tcp_sender_sack_retransmit_probe` establishes **two independent IPv6 TCP Echo flows per implementation**, with the client offering MSS=536 and either SACK-Permitted or no SACK. The raw client injects a 2100-byte application request in multiple TCP segments, validates TCP pseudo-header checksums of every reply and the exact original Echo bytes at each TCP sequence number, then observes at least three distinct outbound server data segments. It cumulatively ACKs the first outgoing segment, treats the next as **lost from its receiver state** (the test has seen it on its raw socket but deliberately never acknowledges it), and builds a SACK range for a **subsequent data segment actually observed**. The SACK mode sends four duplicate TCP ACKs with TCP option kind 5 containing this exact later segment's start/end; the no-SACK control sends otherwise equivalent duplicate ACKs without SACK. This is deterministic raw-client *loss emulation*, not actual physical/link-layer packet dropping, and the test never fabricates SACK for unseen data.

The fixture observes the first missing segment's byte-identical retransmission, records its latency and the number of retransmissions of the SACKed segment *before gap recovery*, and separately records other repeated output. After the hole is recovered, the client **advances its cumulative ACK only over actually observed contiguous TCP data**, receives any remaining segments until the full 2100-byte Echo is present, ACKs that full sequence, and verifies a second fresh Echo message on the **same TCP flow**. Invalid checksums, corrupted retransmission bytes, resets, unrecovered data or failed follow-on Echo are errors. Unique source ports and nftables RST-suppression rules isolate the raw client from its host kernel. No production code, routing, normal TUN path, or shared TCP stack is changed.

The first pinned-oracle comparison observed **2/2 Chimera and 2/2 pinned-Xray** sender flows successfully retransmitting the simulated missing segment, assembling all 2100 bytes and echoing further data, both with and without negotiated SACK. In the tested SACK case Chimera's first observed segments started at offsets **0, 536, 1072**, while the pinned Xray's were **0, 500, 1000**; the latter is an observed segmentation difference, not a correctness failure. Neither stack retransmitted the specifically SACK-reported later segment before the missing segment in this bounded sample. Importantly, the no-SACK controls **also** recovered, so observing a retransmission and data recovery does **not** prove that smoltcp maintains the same selective sender scoreboard as gVisor. The reference gVisor sender contains a SACK scoreboard and RFC 6675 recovery path; smoltcp 0.13.1's established ACK handler uses cumulative ACK, duplicate-ACK fast retransmission (third duplicate), and retransmission timers, without an equivalent sender scoreboard in that path. Treat ACK timing and segmentation differences as diagnostic evidence, not a reason for an unscoped rewrite.

A new vendored integration regression `established_ipv6_tcp_sender_handles_sack_gap_and_cumulative_recovery` runs **four local combinations**, default vs opt-in Xray constructors crossed with SYN SACK-Permitted vs no-SACK. It uses MSS=536, writes 2100 deterministic application bytes to the established netstack stream, ACKs only the first actual outgoing segment, builds a SACK block for a later actually received segment, sends four duplicate ACKs, requires a byte-identical retransmission of the unacknowledged gap, continues cumulatively ACKing **only** observed contiguous bytes through full delivery, and then checks a second write reaches the synthetic client correctly. The test deliberately does **not** demand the same packetization, retransmission count or SACK scoreboard behavior from different TCP stacks. Final verification: `XRAY_BIN=./xray bash scripts/test_xray_tun_routes_netns.sh` **exited 0** with **2/2 Chimera and 2/2 pinned-Xray** sender SACK-on/no-SACK probes and all established receiver-SACK, ACK/RST, Window Scale/zero-window, dual-stack TCP/UDP, XHTTP/H3, ICMP/PMTU, route rollback and shutdown-cleanup checks still passing. The vendor package passed **20 unit and 55 integration** tests; the Chimera TUN library passed **40**. Vendor and workspace strict Clippy for all targets/features (`-D warnings`), `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, Bash syntax, `git diff --check`, and the reduced-feature app `xray-tun` check all passed (the existing **33** reduced-feature warnings remain). This round did not modify production TCP code, commit or clean the existing dirty worktree. Multiple independent missing segments, two SACK ranges, spurious/reneged SACK, DSACK and sender behavior under sustained real packet loss or congestion remain unverified; **Xray TUN stays Partial**.

### 14.10 Two independent outbound TCP holes, two SACK blocks and partial cumulative ACK (2026-10-09)

The pinned oracle remains the installed **Xray 26.9.9 Custom (go1.27rc2 linux/amd64)** at `ref/xray-core` commit `52a412d9e2f5c2a5142b1b4e2ab3771dacb8b120`. This slice extends the real Linux network-namespace **sender-side** SACK loss-emulation probe from 14.09, preserving its original SACK-on/off single-hole baselines. Two further independent raw IPv6 TCP flows per implementation simulate **two nonadjacent missing server→client TCP segments**, with and without SACK negotiation. The client advertises MSS=536, sends a deterministic **3500-byte** Echo request in multiple valid segments, collects at least five distinct byte-for-byte validated server responses, and ACKs only the first contiguous segment. It then explicitly **removes** two nonadjacent segments from its synthetic receive buffer, preserving two actual observed later segments separated by the missing holes; the raw socket may have seen the emulated-lost packets on the wire, but the test never treats those bytes as received by the modeled client.

For SACK-negotiated connections, the client sends **four duplicate cumulative ACKs** at the first hole with a TCP **SACK kind 5/length 18 option containing two genuine noncontiguous [start,end) blocks**, padded with NOP to 20 TCP option bytes. The SACK blocks cover only the two later segments whose valid checksums and payload bytes were actually observed. The non-SACK control sends equivalent duplicate ACKs with **no SACK option**. Each implementation must retransmit the first missing segment with its original sequence and identical payload bytes. The synthetic client then advances the **cumulative ACK only as far as the second hole**, leaving the later received bytes unacknowledged cumulatively; it sends repeated *partial* ACKs with one remaining SACK block (or no SACK, when not negotiated), and requires a retransmission of the second missing segment, regardless of whether that retransmission arrived before or after the partial ACK. The test records the observed gap retransmission order, validates each original payload byte and packet checksum, never acknowledges beyond the actual contiguous receive buffer, fully drains/ACKs the **3500-byte** Echo and successfully exchanges a **second fresh application payload on the same connection**. Valid packet content and eventual recovery are hard assertions; exact TCP segmentation, retransmission count, fast-recovery mode and RTO timing are not assumed identical across gVisor and smoltcp.

In the first real pinned-oracle run, **4/4 Chimera and 4/4 Xray** sender-side flows passed across the retained single-hole (2) and new double-hole (2) cases. Both SACK-enabled two-hole implementations retransmitted the gap offsets in observed order **[first, second]** with actual partial cumulative ACK. Chimera's first five outbound segment offsets were **[0,536,1072,1608,2144]**, while Xray/gVisor with SACK used **[0,500,1000,1500,2000]** and without SACK used **[0,536,1000,1536,2072]**; both maintained the exact original application byte stream despite those segmentation differences. Non-SACK controls also recovered, so these results show *correct double-hole and partial-ACK recovery*, not proof that the sender's SACK scoreboard utilization or optimized loss-retransmission policy matches Xray.

The new vendored integration regression `established_ipv6_tcp_sender_two_sack_holes_recover_after_partial_ack` asserts the same two-hole lifecycle under four independent combinations of ordinary/default and Xray-compatible netstack constructors crossed with SACK on/off. It writes deterministic **3500 bytes** with MSS=536, captures five actual outbound segments, validates two noncontiguous SACK blocks, removes both emulated-lost packets from the synthetic receive buffer, requires the first gap's valid retransmission, deliberately issues a partial cumulative ACK stopping at the second gap with only the later SACK block, requires the second gap retransmission, drains/ACKs the full exact byte stream and verifies a subsequent application write. The test is independent of physical packet-loss timing, and does not claim DSACK, three or more holes, SACK reneging, large-scale congestion control parity, or a complete gVisor sender-scoreboard implementation. Final verification: `XRAY_BIN=./xray bash scripts/test_xray_tun_routes_netns.sh` **exited 0**, with **4/4 Chimera and 4/4 pinned-Xray** single/double-hole sender cases plus existing receiver SACK, ACK/RST, IPv6 fragment, Window Scale/zero-window, dual-stack TCP/UDP, XHTTP/H3, ICMP/PMTU, route rollback and shutdown tests. The vendor package passed **20 unit and 56 integration** tests, including the new four-combination double-hole regression; `cargo test -p chimera_server_lib --lib --all-features tun_gateway --locked` passed **40**. Vendor and workspace all-target/all-feature strict Clippy with `-D warnings`, Rust formatting, Bash syntax, `git diff --check`, and the reduced-feature `xray-tun` app check passed (the existing **33** reduced-feature warnings remain). The first live iteration rejected an exactly adjacent SACK range/next-hole boundary as overlapping; the fixture was corrected to permit adjacent nonoverlapping ranges before the final, source-frozen full smoke run. No production TCP runtime code is changed, no Git commit is created and no worktree cleanup is performed; **Xray TUN remains Partial**.

### 14.11 SACK reneging and genuine duplicate-SACK (DSACK) on established IPv6 TCP (2026-10-09)

Pinned oracle is the same fixed **Xray 26.9.9 Custom (go1.27rc2 linux/amd64)**, `ref/xray-core` commit `52a412d9e2f5c2a5142b1b4e2ab3771dacb8b120`. This iteration adds **two new SACK-only established TCP sender scenarios per implementation** to `run_ipv6_tcp_sender_sack_retransmit_probe`, preserving the earlier single-hole and two-hole SACK-on/off baselines (six independent connections per TUN). Both are synthetically orchestrated TCP receiver behaviors over the real Linux network namespace, **not** real link-layer packet loss or resource-memory-pressure tests. Each raw packet uses the correct IPv6/TCP pseudo-header checksum, a negotiated SACK-Permitted SYN and the real Echo application's TCP stream. Exact Xray/smoltcp retransmission intervals and native sender SACK scoreboard contents are not asserted equal.

**Reneging:** request a deterministic **2100-byte Echo**, capture three actual outbound segments at validated sequence numbers and bytes, cumulatively ACK the first, report the *genuinely received* third segment in a TCP SACK option kind 5 while the second is missing, and transmit four duplicate ACKs. The synthetic receiver then explicitly discards its copy of the SACKed third segment, modelling RFC 2018 SACK reneging under receiver memory pressure; after that it sends **no further SACK** for the discarded range. It accepts a retransmission of the second (original) gap, then sends cumulative ACKs **only over actually contiguous retained/re-received data**, stopping at the reneged third segment until that exact range arrives again. It requires byte-for-byte restoration of all 2100 application bytes and a second fresh Echo on the same connection. This distinguishes a sender that permanently assumes SACK implies delivery from one that eventually recovers after the receiver discards previously selectively acknowledged data. The pinned gVisor implementation explicitly resets its sender SACK scoreboard on retransmission timeout (`tcp/snd.go`, `retransmitTimerExpired`); smoltcp 0.13.1's sender uses cumulative ACK/duplicate ACK/RTO without an analogous sender scoreboard in this path. Their implementations need not match for the recovery safety property to hold.

**DSACK:** establish a separate SACK-Permitted TCP flow and request a small Echo, then **withhold its cumulative ACK until the raw client has actually received two byte-identical TCP data segments at the same sequence** (a genuine server retransmission of previously received data). Only *after* witnessing both copies does it send a cumulative ACK covering the data **with a valid SACK kind 5 block [segment_start,segment_end) strictly below that new cumulative ACK**, the RFC 2883 DSACK shape. The test then immediately sends another fresh TCP application payload and requires a correct Echo with no reset, stale sequence confusion or lost data. An arbitrary SACK block below ACK without a duplicate would be a fabricated DSACK, so it is explicitly forbidden by the test. The first live comparison showed the duplicate after about **1.0 second for Chimera** and **0.2 second for pinned Xray**; these are controlled topology observations only, not RTO guarantees.

The vendored integration regression `established_ipv6_tcp_sack_reneging_and_dsack_preserve_stream` runs **both default `tun-gateway` and opt-in Xray-TUN netstack constructors**. It negotiates MSS 536+SACK Permitted, writes a deterministic 2100-byte payload, asserts the synthetically lost second segment and later SACKed third segment are both re-received with unchanged content after renouncing that SACK, ACKs only actually contiguous bytes, and tests a genuine DSACK on a second application write after observing two matching outbound copies. A final third write must still travel over the same stream. The regression checks TCP SYN/SYN-ACK options and resets, actual TCP sequence and payload, the true duplicate condition and later successful data; it does not assert how the server scores SACK, whether DSACK changes its congestion window or RTT estimator, or equivalence under a prolonged resource/congestion failure. No production TCP code or shared default netstack behavior was changed. Final validation: `XRAY_BIN=./xray bash scripts/test_xray_tun_routes_netns.sh` **exited 0**, with **6/6 Chimera and 6/6 pinned-Xray** sender SACK flows (the original single-/two-hole on/off controls, reneging, and genuine duplicate-triggered DSACK). All existing receiver SACK, IPv6 fragmentation/ACK/RST, TCP FIN, zero-window/Window Scale, IPv4/IPv6 TCP/UDP, XHTTP/H3, ICMP/PMTU, route rollback and shutdown cleanup checks passed. The vendor crate passed **20 unit and 57 integration** tests, including the new default/Xray-opt-in reneging/DSACK regression; `cargo test -p chimera_server_lib --lib --all-features tun_gateway --locked` passed **40**. Vendor/workspace strict all-target/all-feature Clippy (`-D warnings`), Rust formatting, Bash syntax and `git diff --check`, plus the reduced-feature `xray-tun` app check passed (the pre-existing **33** reduced-feature warnings remain). No production TCP/runtime code was changed, and no Git commit or worktree cleanup was performed. DSACK response *generation* by the receiver, forged/invalid DSACK ranges, high-load reneging and SACK reneging with multiple simultaneous holes remain unverified. **Xray TUN stays Partial**.

### 14.12 TCP Timestamp negotiation, old TSval on new data, and old-sequence replay (2026-10-09)

Pinned external oracle: the locally installed **Xray 26.9.9 Custom (go1.27rc2 linux/amd64)**, `ref/xray-core` commit `52a412d9e2f5c2a5142b1b4e2ab3771dacb8b120`, using pinned gVisor. The new `run_ipv6_tcp_timestamp_replay_probe` adds **two independent established raw IPv6 TCP connections per TUN** with a SYN offering RFC 7323 Timestamp (`TSval=40000, TSecr=0`). The SYN-ACK's *actual* TCP options are parsed and validated: the current vendored smoltcp 0.13.1 socket does **not** install a `TcpTimestampGenerator` and thus **does not negotiate Timestamp**, while the fixed Xray/gVisor **does negotiate** and echoes the client's SYN TSval in SYN-ACK TSecr. The raw test sends subsequent Timestamp options **only when Timestamp was mutually negotiated**. Chimera instead receives normal NOP-padded TCP packets; accepting such a packet is not evidence of PAWS behavior.

The control first sends a valid 41000-TSval (or unnegotiated plain) payload and verifies the exact Echo. Then the raw client sends a **new TCP sequence** carrying previously unused application bytes but a **stale TSval=40001**, lower than the accepted 41000. This is different from an old-sequence duplicate, which any TCP implementation should ignore regardless of PAWS. The stale-TS payload travels in **out-of-order, two-fragment IPv6 packets** with one or two padding-only Destination Options headers after Fragment and with only eight TCP header bytes in fragment zero. The original TCP pseudo-header checksum, IPv6 fragment offsets and TCP option lengths are valid. If the negotiated-TS peer drops this stale-TS data, the fixture retransmits the *same unacknowledged TCP sequence and application bytes* under a fresh IPv6 fragment ID with a newer valid `TSval=42000`, and requires complete recovery; if the old TS was accepted, it still validates that exact Echo, without incorrectly resending previously acknowledged data.

**Observed pinned Xray 26.9.9 behavior:** in **both** IPv6 extension-header layouts, Xray negotiated Timestamp **and accepted the new-sequence data with old TSval=40001**; the Echo was checksum-valid and byte-exact. This particular gVisor version's `tcp/rcv.go` `handleTimeWaitSegment` explicitly states PAWS is not yet supported in the TIME_WAIT case, while `endpoint.go` `updateRecentTimestamp` updates recent timestamps only on monotonic advance. The measured established-data acceptance therefore must not be described as strict RFC 7323 PAWS rejection: **negotiating TCP Timestamp is not proof that the pinned Xray enforces PAWS stale-data rejection**. Chimera's two layouts also recovered new data, but *only on non-Timestamp fallback packets* because its SYN-ACK declined the option. There is no valid measured Chimera PAWS-enabled path in these tests; enabling one would be an independent production semantic change rather than a compatibility patch justified by these observations.

After the stale-TS case, both fixtures ACK the exact cumulative bytes received and inject a **replay of the previously accepted TCP segment using its old TCP sequence**, under a **new fragment ID** (so fragment reassembly caching cannot hide the duplicate). In Xray this replay carries a newer TSval; in Chimera it carries no Timestamp because none was negotiated. **Neither** implementation delivered a duplicate Echo. Another fresh in-order application payload with valid negotiated options then echoed correctly in both implementations. Every server TCP reply is validated for IPv6/TCP pseudo-header checksum, four-tuple, flags and TCP Data Offset. The two cases per implementation protect replay suppression, established-session continuity and IPv6 extension/fragment handling; they do not claim handling of timestamp wraparound, PAWS TSrecent expiry after 24 days, pathological delay/reordering, middlebox timestamp changes, timestamp option disappearance midstream, TIME_WAIT SYN recycling or successful PAWS *rejection* parity.

The new vendored regression `established_ipv6_tcp_timestamp_decline_replay_and_recovery` runs **four local cases** (ordinary/default and Xray-opt-in netstack constructors × one/two Destination Options headers). It sends a valid Timestamp-offering SYN, explicitly requires that the SYN-ACK does **not** negotiate Timestamp with the current default smoltcp socket configuration, then sends real application TCP bytes **without unnegotiated Timestamp options**, replays the *same sequence and bytes* under a fresh IPv6 Fragment ID, verifies the application stream does **not** receive duplicate bytes, and verifies further fresh data on the same flow. Final verification: the strengthened, source-frozen `XRAY_BIN=./xray bash scripts/test_xray_tun_routes_netns.sh` **exited 0**, asserting **2/2 Chimera and 2/2 pinned-Xray** Timestamp-negotiation/stale-TS/new-sequence/old-sequence-replay/fresh-continuation cases, plus all existing sender/receiver SACK, two-hole/reneging/DSACK, TCP ACK/RST/FIN, zero-window/Window Scale, IPv4/IPv6 TCP/UDP, XHTTP/H3, ICMP/PMTU, route rollback and clean-shutdown checks. The vendor crate passed **20 unit and 58 integration** tests including the new four-layout Timestamp-decline/duplicate-replay regression; `cargo test -p chimera_server_lib --lib --all-features tun_gateway --locked` passed **40**. Vendor and workspace all-target/all-feature strict Clippy with `-D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `bash -n`, `git diff --check`, and reduced-feature `xray-tun` app build all passed (the pre-existing **33** reduced-feature warnings remain). This slice adds no production TCP sender/receiver logic and does not alter the default site-gateway path, create a Git commit or clean the worktree. **Xray TUN stays Partial**.

### 14.13 RFC 7323 TSval wraparound, TSecr echo and missing Timestamp after SYN negotiation (2026-10-09)

The external oracle remains the pinned local **Xray 26.9.9 Custom (go1.27rc2 linux/amd64)**, `ref/xray-core` commit `52a412d9e2f5c2a5142b1b4e2ab3771dacb8b120` using pinned gVisor. This reviewable extension to `run_ipv6_tcp_timestamp_replay_probe` preserves the two ordinary 14.12 TCP Timestamp test connections and adds two **new, independent** IPv6 TCP flows per implementation, one with a single and one with a double padding-only Destination Options chain after Fragment. Each uses a raw SYN with Timestamp **TSval=`0xfffffff0`**, checks SYN-ACK option negotiation and the exact SYN TSecr, then sends new-sequence data at TSval **`0xfffffff8`** followed by data at **`0x00000008`** on the same established connection. This is a positive monotonic increment under 32-bit sequence-space wraparound, not a stale timestamp. The second data payload, and later tests, are carried as out-of-order IPv6 fragments with exactly **eight TCP header bytes** in fragment zero; original TCP checksums and IPv6 offsets are valid. All server TCP replies are checked for checksum, four-tuple, TCP Data Offset and absence of reset; Echo payloads are validated byte for byte.

**Negotiation asymmetry is essential.** The current vendored smoltcp 0.13.1 listener does not install `TcpTimestampGenerator` in either the default or Xray-TUN-compatible constructor; it **declines Timestamp in SYN-ACK** even when SYN offers `0xfffffff0`. The client therefore uses regular packets **without unnegotiated Timestamp** throughout Chimera's subsequent data-plane flow. That path validates connection continuity and TCP sequence replay suppression, **not** RFC 7323 PAWS on Chimera. By contrast fixed Xray 26.9.9 **negotiates TCP Timestamp** in both layouts and reflects the SYN TSval in SYN-ACK TSecr. After Xray receives client TSval `0xfffffff8`, it accepts fresh in-order data at `0x00000008`, and Echo TSecr reflects those actual values. The preliminary live comparison observed the same behavior in both one/two-chain cases. The `endpoint.go` `updateRecentTimestamp` helper uses serial-number ordering to update RecentTS across wrap boundaries. This is a bounded, **seconds-long wrap transition** constructed with test-only values, not evidence of real long-lived timestamp clock rollover, PAWS age protection, or TIME_WAIT timestamp behavior.

After the wrap, the raw client injects a **fresh-sequence data segment whose TCP header intentionally omits Timestamp**, despite Xray having negotiated it in SYN/SYN-ACK. The test records whether the receiver accepts this anomalous segment. If rejected, it retransmits **the same still-unacknowledged TCP sequence** with a valid newer Timestamp option and a new IPv6 fragment ID; it never fabricates successful delivery or skips unreceived bytes.
