**Measured on real Linux TUN.** Chimera's two independent connections both reached real zero with server Window Scale **3** after **1,196,024 accepted bytes each** and completed the synchronized cohort successfully. The pinned Xray 26.9.9's two connections reached real zero at **999,424** and **4,913,152** bytes respectively, both at server Window Scale **8**; this large intra-implementation difference confirms buffer pressure thresholds are not appropriate assertions and may reflect gVisor flow-specific buffering/scheduling. Both Xray workers passed the synchronized barrier, rejected-probe/ACK checks, reopened, and acknowledged the valid retransmission and subsequent gap-repair/fresh data with **TSecr 50000 → 50010 → 50020**. On **each** of the four stressed connections, normal TCP echo isolation passed **4/4 while stalled** plus **4/4 after reopening**, producing 32 additional independently completed echo sessions total across both implementations. The experiment shows **two simultaneously stalled server receive windows recover independently**; it does **not** demonstrate random packet loss, more than two simultaneous stalled connections, retransmission backoff equivalence, large-scale resource exhaustion or PAWS enforcement.

**Isolation and execution.** No production Rust code, reference binary/source, default `tun-gateway`, existing `XRAY_TUN_SERVER_RX_PRESSURE=1` single-stream oracle, or routine namespace smoke is changed. Only the already-untracked explicit namespace fixture and compatibility docs are extended; `XRAY_TUN_SERVER_RX_MULTISTALL=1` opts into the two-worker mode while `XRAY_TUN_SERVER_RX_PRESSURE=1` retains its previous single-worker mode. Each worker keeps the existing 20 MiB/100 s pressure bounds, the new barrier has a 35-second limit and each worker verifies exact TSecr/ACK outcomes. The ordinary vendored netstack unit/integration tests and the Chimera TUN library suite remain separate regression gates. **The overall Xray TUN feature stays Partial** because this one controlled two-stream combination cannot prove arbitrary concurrent receiver stalls, genuine seeded network loss, gVisor autotuning fidelity or full PAWS equivalence.

**Final verification.** The entire `XRAY_TUN_SERVER_RX_MULTISTALL=1 XRAY_BIN=./xray bash scripts/test_xray_tun_routes_netns.sh` namespace suite **exited 0**. Both implementations each produced **two** independently successful zero-window and recovery reports plus a mandatory `multistall cohort=2 both-recovered=True` report; every worker passed the held-window rejected-payload checks, four independent normal TCP echoes while stalled, four more echoes after reopening, and the `50000 → 50010 → 50020` TSecr sequence. The surrounding suite also passed all **eight Timestamp** and **eight Blackhole/TIME_WAIT** reports, double-stack TCP/UDP, XHTTP H3/QUIC, ICMP/PMTU, route-conflict rollback and strict device/route cleanup. `cargo test --manifest-path vendor/watfaq-netstack/Cargo.toml` passed **25 unit and 64 ordinary integration** tests (two expensive tests still ignored by default); `cargo test -p chimera_server_lib --lib --all-features tun_gateway --locked` passed **40** tests; strict workspace Clippy, both Rustfmt checks, Bash syntax and Git diff whitespace checks passed. The explicit single-zero-window and **512 active-socket capacity** tests also passed separately. No production path was touched.

### 14.31 Bounded real Linux seeded `tc netem` loss and independent 8 KiB downlink regression (2026-10-11)

**Reference and on-wire test.** Continue with pinned `ref/xray-core` at `52a412d9e2f5c2a5142b1b4e2ab3771dacb8b120` and `./xray` 26.9.9 Custom (go1.27rc2 linux/amd64). The new opt-in `XRAY_TUN_SEEDED_NETEM=1 XRAY_BIN=./xray bash scripts/test_xray_tun_routes_netns.sh` creates a **new** `tc qdisc` on **only** the disposable Office client's `office-client` veth egress. It uses Linux kernel `netem loss random 3% seed 31991`; each implementation gets a freshly initialized independent qdisc with the same seed and no persistent host/default-namespace network changes. Four **real kernel TCP** IPv6 streams concurrently connect to the existing TCP/39643 echo service, each exchange 128 KiB of fixed reproducible data in 1 KiB request/response chunks, and verify SHA-256 integrity. The script reads qdisc `tc -s -j` counters and Linux `TCP_INFO.tcpi_total_retrans` (kernel UAPI offset 100), requiring **at least one actual qdisc drop and one actual TCP retransmission**. It then removes the qdisc in `finally`, verifies there is no netem left, and requires an additional successful post-loss TCP echo before resuming the existing raw-Timestamp, UDP, QUIC, routing and teardown probes. Failed cases are recorded for each implementation separately and the entire namespace suite exits nonzero after cleanup. The test is off by default and never applies qdisc changes in the developer's host network namespace.

**Measured bounded parity.** The passing paired 3%/1 KiB real-Linux-TUN test produced **23 qdisc drops** and client-side retransmissions **[4, 11, 4, 3]** for Chimera, and **24 drops** and **[4, 6, 3, 11]** for pinned Xray. All **4/4 flows on each implementation** returned the exact hashed bytes, and both independently removed the temporary qdisc and passed a fresh echo. Matching the random seed does not force identical per-packet loss identities: sender segmentation, pacing, scheduler timing and the qdisc's per-attachment RNG input sequence differ between implementations. This proves a specific bounded, authentic drop-and-retransmit combination, **not** general congestion control or stochastic loss-burst equivalence.

**Important separate failure: large single-write downlink.** The *initial* 6% loss / 256 KiB per flow / single bulk `sendall`-then-read test **failed on Chimera** (first receive timed out at 30 s) but **passed pinned Xray** (43 qdisc drops and per-flow TCP retransmissions [15, 0, 0, 25]). This is not valid evidence of a pure loss-recovery divergence because the initial application schedule could itself deadlock behind a full-duplex buffer. In an explicit **zero-loss, no-netem** Chimera-only isolation run, however, **one single 8 KiB send** at TCP/39643 still failed to receive its echo, while the destination's actual TCP service logged receipt and echo of **two 4096-byte chunks**. Its Linux client `TCP_INFO` reported zero unacknowledged segments and zero retransmissions when the receive timed out. The same 8 KiB sent/retrieved in **eight 1 KiB chunks** succeeded, as did a 128-byte control. Setting `CHIMERA_TCP_RELAY_BACKEND=copy` did **not** resolve the single-8 KiB real-namespace timeout, so attributing it solely to the default `handoff` relay would be unsound. Separately, the existing ignored true-server-zero-window in-memory netstack test was extended to write 8 KiB into the app-side `TcpStream` **in one call**, and verified that the smoltcp output produced ordered, MTU-bounded TCP segments and all 8192 bytes. The actual remaining fault is therefore **somewhere in the end-to-end Chimera downlink/network path**, not proven to be smoltcp's base 8 KiB segmentation. No speculative production fix was made. Future work must use packet-level on-wire capture and/or relay TX notifications to locate whether the large echo leaves the TUN device with valid TCP sequence/checksum; maintain this as an explicit **Partial / open regression** rather than silently treating smaller chunks as a complete fix.

**Diagnostic isolation.** The optional `XRAY_TUN_SEEDED_NETEM_CHIMERA_ONLY=1` stops after the Chimera side, explicitly reporting that the full paired suite was *not* run; the bounded diagnostic variables `XRAY_TUN_SEEDED_NETEM_LOSS_PCT=0|1|3`, `XRAY_TUN_SEEDED_FLOW_COUNT=1|4`, `XRAY_TUN_SEEDED_PAYLOAD_BYTES=128|8192|131072` and `XRAY_TUN_SEEDED_CHUNK_BYTES=128|1024|8192` can reproduce the no-loss large-write control without affecting default behavior. The full paired reference always uses the documented default 3%/four 128 KiB flows/1 KiB chunks. No production Rust, reference code or default `tun-gateway` path changed in this work. **Xray TUN remains Partial.**

**Final quality and immutable-script execution.** After the opt-in fixture was finalized and no longer edited during execution, the complete `XRAY_TUN_SEEDED_NETEM=1 XRAY_BIN=./xray bash scripts/test_xray_tun_routes_netns.sh` **exited 0**. Its final run independently measured **23 qdisc drops / TCP retransmissions [3, 9, 5, 5]** for Chimera and **24 drops / retransmissions [6, 11, 4, 3]** for pinned Xray; all eight 128 KiB echoes passed SHA-256, both qdiscs were removed and fresh post-loss echoes succeeded. The full suite additionally passed **8 Timestamp** and **8 Blackhole/TIME_WAIT** reports plus existing IPv4/IPv6, UDP, QUIC/H3, PMTU, route rollback and device/route teardown checks. One prior trial of the passing 3% case printed the final success banner but **exited nonzero with a Bash parser error**, because the test script was being edited while its Bash process was still reading it; that trial is **not** counted as a passing full suite. The definitive no-concurrent-edit rerun above passed cleanly. Vendored tests: **25 unit + 64 standard integration passed, two high-resource cases ignored by default**. The separately invoked true-server-zero-window integration test, expanded with the 8 KiB packetized downlink, passed. Chimera TUN library tests: **40 passed**. Strict workspace Clippy, Rustfmt for workspace and vendor, Bash syntax and `git diff --check` passed. The standalone no-netem 8 KiB end-to-end failure remains an independent open regression; **do not promote `xray-tun` beyond Partial**.

### 14.32 Xray-only TCP full-MSS Timestamp overflow fix (2026-10-11)

**Observed failure and root cause.** The open, zero-loss 8 KiB single-write downlink stall from §14.31 is reproduced at packet level. An isolated read-only AF_PACKET capture on office-gw, cxray-tun and echo-gw showed the client sent and the downstream application received/echoed all 8192 bytes, but Chimera emitted only the last **152 bytes** starting at TCP SEQ **server_seq + 8040**. Exactly six initial **1340-byte** segments never reached the client, and the server repeatedly retransmitted the tail. The pinned smoltcp **0.13.1** TCP dispatch calculates outgoing data MSS using IP MTU minus the base IP and **20-byte** TCP header, ignoring the extra Timestamp/SACK options. Thus an IPv6 TCP data packet with 1340 bytes of payload plus 40 IPv6 + 20 basic TCP + 12 Timestamp is **1412 bytes**, beyond the real 1400 MTU. The smoltcp interface drops these oversized packets even though the TCP send cursor has advanced. A new non-ignored in-memory regression, **xray_tcp_timestamp_large_peer_mss_segments_fit_tun_mtu**, uses SYN MSS 1460 and TSval with MTU 1400 and reproduces the *same 8040-byte sequence gap* before the fix.

**Minimal fix.** Merely reducing the virtual smoltcp device MTU by 12 does *not* work: both the outgoing MSS calculation and the transmitter's MTU validation then decrease by the same amount, leaving an identical option overflow. Therefore, only in the explicit **Xray TUN** packet pump, after the incoming SYN has passed its original checksum validation, an **internal-only copy** of the peer SYN has any oversized MSS option reduced to at most **configured MTU minus IPv4/IPv6 base IP header minus 20-byte TCP header minus a 40-byte maximum TCP option budget**. The TCP checksum of that private copy is recomputed before injection into smoltcp. At MTU 1400, the resulting maximum is **1300 for IPv6** or **1320 for IPv4**. An absent or malformed MSS is not synthesized, valid smaller MSS is unchanged, and any retransmitted SYN receives the same treatment. The external wire SYN, client options, actual TUN MTU, UDP processing, peer-visible SYN-ACK and ordinary **tun-gateway** all stay unchanged. This conservative limit also affects Xray clients without Timestamp, a bounded MSS/throughput tradeoff because smoltcp does not expose a per-socket local MSS setter; no smoltcp fork or new dependency was added.

**Deterministic red-to-green evidence.** The new non-ignored real-MSS in-memory regression failed with an 8040-byte gap before the fix and now passes with contiguous TCP sequence, complete 8192-byte content and all emitted IPv6 packets at or below the actual configured MTU. A unit regression checks correct **1300/1320** internal MSS values for IPv6/IPv4, original SYN not mutated, preserved Timestamp and SACK/window options, recomputed TCP checksum and idempotent no-op for an already-safe MSS. The original real Linux one-send 8192-byte failure was rerun with **zero netem**, one flow, 1400 MTU and optional read-only capture: it **exited 0** with exact SHA-256 data integrity. The capture now shows **six 1300-byte data segments plus a 392-byte tail**, all correctly sequenced and at most **1372 bytes** each including IPv6/TCP headers and Timestamp; no 8040-byte gap or repeated tail. The opt-in **XRAY_TUN_BULK_ECHO=1** mode adds this same one-shot 8 KiB real Linux check independently for Chimera and the pinned Xray **26.9.9** reference, and may be combined with **XRAY_TUN_SEEDED_NETEM=1** for the existing 3%-loss, four-flow probe. The diagnostic AF_PACKET reader is **scripts/diagnose_xray_tun_tcp_capture.py**, used only inside the disposable namespace, with no tcpdump dependency. Linux veth packet-capture checksum indicators may reflect checksum offload, while TUN-injected outputs were independently verified.

**Boundaries.** This P0 repair fixes the reproduced TCP Timestamp full-MSS gap. It does not assert exact gVisor MSS advertising, congestion control, SACK retransmit pacing, full PAWS, all physical MTUs or sustained high-load parity. The overall Xray TUN compatibility status remains **Partial**.

**Final verified paired regression.** With the script finalized and not edited while running, `XRAY_TUN_BULK_ECHO=1 XRAY_TUN_SEEDED_NETEM=1 XRAY_BIN=./xray bash scripts/test_xray_tun_routes_netns.sh` **exited 0**. Both Chimera and pinned Xray 26.9.9 passed the zero-loss, one-shot **8192-byte TCP downlink** with exact SHA-256 integrity and post-test clean echo. Both also passed four **128 KiB** kernel TCP echoes at **3% netem packet loss, seed 31991**; the observed qdisc drops were **23/23** and Linux client TCP retransmissions **[5,3,4,11] / [8,3,2,6]**, followed by qdisc removal and successful fresh echo. The rest of the real namespace run passed **8 Timestamp** and **8 Blackhole/TIME_WAIT** reports, IPv4/IPv6 TCP/UDP, SACK/fragment options, XHTTP H3/QUIC, ICMP/PMTU, route-conflict rollback and final TUN and route cleanup. The netstack passed **26 unit + 65 normal integration tests**, with both expensive single-receive-zero-window and 512-active-socket tests **run explicitly and passed**; Chimera TUN library tests passed **40**. Strict all-workspace Clippy, Rustfmt, Bash syntax and `git diff --check` passed. The failed original 8 KiB case and the passing post-fix AF_PACKET traces are retained as separate evidence in the recovery snapshot; default `tun-gateway` and reference sources were not modified.

### 14.33 3x-ui `/debug/vars` metrics listener compatibility (2026-10-11)

The Xray-shaped top-level `metrics.listen` now owns an HTTP listener at `/debug/vars`, started and supervised with the server. The response includes nested traffic counters, active outbound Observatory entries, and the `memstats` field names consumed by the 3x-ui sampler. `metrics.tag` with `listen` remains accepted with an explicit warning because the tagged outbound path is not implemented; tag-only configurations fail validation. Rust does not have Go heap/GC counters: Linux RSS and virtual size are exposed in the legacy `HeapAlloc`/`Sys` slots as memory-history approximations, `HeapObjects`/`NumGC`/`PauseNs` are zero, and a `chimera` marker states these values are not Go runtime equivalents. Non-Linux process-memory collection is not implemented. The process e2e using `examples/xray-compatible/3x-ui-default-api.json5` verifies HTTP JSON fields and existing gRPC Stats/Routing calls; targeted config tests, all-feature integration-target Clippy, formatting, and the actual 3x-ui template `--check` passed. This is endpoint-level Chimera evidence, not a run of the 3x-ui Go sampler or exact Xray allocator/GC parity.
